# AGENTS.md

## Tooling rules (MUST follow)

- **NEVER use `python3 -` / heredoc / sed scripts via Bash to edit files.** All file edits MUST go through the built-in **Edit** and **Write** tools, and file reading through **Read/Grep/Glob**. Bash is for build/test/git commands only. This has been explicitly requested by the user more than once — do not regress.
- **ALL tests/probes of the built binary MUST run with `VIMRUNTIME=runtime`.** Without it the dev binary E484s on missing runtime files (e.g. `/syntax/syntax.v`) and exits 1 for environmental reasons; the installed ref binary has its runtime baked in. This applies to gates, `-S` probes, and TUI runs alike.

## Session start: always read these first

Before doing anything else in a new session, read:
1. `src/odin/PHASE0_FINDINGS.md` — build process, linker flags, symbol visibility, tracking system
2. `ODIN_PORT.md` — Neovim architecture overview, port strategy, Odin patterns, known blockers

- **Ported `os/proc.c` to Odin**: 3 functions in `src/odin/os_proc.odin` (`os_proc_tree_kill`, `os_proc_children`, `os_proc_running`; Linux path only — MSWin/BSD/Apple branches dropped). `uv_kill` is FFI'd via `foreign _` (`@(link_name="uv_kill")`, linked through `-luv`). `UV_ESRCH` defined as `c.int(-c.int(posix.ESRCH))` (Linux) / `-4040` (other). `os_proc_children` reads `/proc/<ppid>/task/<ppid>/children` with `libc.fopen`/`fscanf`/`fclose` (`#c_vararg` fscanf handles `^c.int` correctly — no `..any` boxing issue), parses ints into a `[dynamic]c.int`, then hands an `xmalloc`'d `^c.int` to the caller (C-side frees via `xfree`). `proc.h` was KEPT in `src/nvim/os/` (only `proc.c` moved to `bak/os/proc.c`); `CMakeLists` globs `os/*.c` so dropping `proc.c` removes the stale `.o` — must `cmake --build build --target libnvim` to rebuild `libnvim.a`. Moved `src/nvim/os/proc.c` → `bak/os/proc.c`. Gate passes; `jobstart(['echo','hello_proc'])` returns jobid and captures stdout, exercising `os_proc_running`/`os_proc_tree_kill`.
- **Completed `event/` (libuv event loop) port to Odin** — keep libuv as the engine (FFI via `-luv`), reimplement Neovim-specific logic in Odin; C `.h` prototypes kept so ~20 C consumers keep linking against Odin `@(export)` symbols. **Done**: `event/multiqueue.c` → `multiqueue.odin` + `event_defs.odin`; `event/loop.c` → `loop.odin` + `uv_defs.odin`; `event/signal.c` → `signal.odin`; `os/signal.c` → `os_signal.odin`; `event/stream.c`/`rstream.c`/`wstream.c`/`socket.c` → `stream.odin`/`rstream.odin`/`wstream.odin`/`socket.odin`; **`event/proc.c` → `proc.odin` + `event/libuv_proc.c` → `libuv_proc.odin`** (all `@(export)` symbols: `proc_spawn`/`proc_teardown`/`proc_stop`/`proc_wait`/`proc_free`/`decref`/`proc_close`/`flush_stream`/`on_proc_exit`/`on_proc_stream_close`/`proc_close_handles`/`exit_event`/`exit_on_closed_chan`, plus `libuv_proc_init`/`libuv_proc_stdio`/`libuv_proc_spawn`/`libuv_proc_close`/`exit_cb`). C sources moved to `bak/`. `main_loop` is now an Odin `@(export)` global. Gate + runtime pass: `--version` (`NVIM v0.13.0-dev`), `-u NONE -c qa!`, `jobstart(['echo',..])` (on_stdout fires with real child pid), `timer_start`, signals (SIGWINCH/SIGTERM/etc. handled, no "invalid signal"), and `--listen` TCP server with RPC client both exit clean. **Gotchas (this Odin build, dev-2026-07)**: (a) **`union` not supported** — use plain `rawptr` fields + cast helpers. (b) **enum constants are `Enum.member`** (e.g. `uv_run_mode.UV_RUN_ONCE`). (c) **`proc` keyword** can't be a param name (`proc:` illegal → renamed to `p`). (d) **`^rawptr` not indexable** — pointer arithmetic via `uintptr`. (e) **forward-type recursion**: all event types must live in ONE file (`event_defs.odin`). (f) libuv handle structs mirrored as leading-`data: rawptr` + sized `_pad`; sizes from `sizeof`. (g) **`Loop.children` must store `Proc*` POINTERS** (matching C `kvec_t(Proc*)`), NOT `Proc` values — modeled as `items: ^rawptr` (Odin has no `^^T`), element `[i]` cast `^Proc`. Storing `Proc` copies broke `decref`'s kvec lookup (assertion `i < kv_children_size`). (h) **`uv_process_t` layout**: `pid` is at offset **104** (`exit_cb@96` between `loop@8` and `pid`), NOT 100 — our struct must include `exit_cb: rawptr` before `pid` or `pr.pid` reads 0 (caused `proc_stop`/`os_proc_tree_kill(0,...)`). Verified via `__builtin_offsetof(uv_process_t, pid)` = 104; `sizeof(uv_process_t)` = 136. (i) **`proc_teardown` must drain `loop.events` BEFORE the blocking `loop_poll_events(loop,-1)`** each iteration (matching C's `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS_UNTIL`); otherwise stuck `loop.events` never drain and `uv_run(UV_RUN_ONCE)` blocks forever → `--version`/`-c qa!` hang. (j) **`uv_stdio_container_t` MUST be 16 bytes** (flags@0 + 4 pad + data:u64@8); model as `flags: u32, _pad: u32, data: u64`. Originally a nested struct laid out to 24 → libuv strides stdio array by 16 reading wrong flags. (k) **`signal_start` TEMP `if true { return }` debug guard** was removed after verification. (l) **No `var`/`goto` in Odin** — `proc`/`in`/`out` are reserved keywords; struct fields renamed `in_s`/`out_s`/`err_s`; use `x := val` / `x: T = val`.
- **Replaced `__attribute__((weak))` with `#pragma weak`** in `memory.c` — LPEG grammar can't parse `__attribute__` before return type, causing generated declarations to vanish. `#pragma weak` is a preprocessor directive the grammar handles.
- **Added leak tracking with `backtrace` + `dladdr`**: `capture_caller()` captures `bt[2]` (C caller return address) into `AllocInfo{size, caller}`. On exit, `dladdr` resolves each caller to `function+offset`. Supports known-wrapper skipping (`alloc_block`, `ga_grow`, `xmallocz`, `xstrdup`, `xmemdupz`, `xstrnsave`, `xrealloc`) to reveal actual callers.
- **Perf fix (critical)**: `xmalloc`/`xfree`/`xcalloc`/`xrealloc`/`try_malloc`/`verbose_try_malloc` previously called `capture_caller()` (`backtrace()`+`dladdr()`) on **every** allocation — millions of calls on the hot path — making the editor hang (~120s). Now the `alloc_sizes` **size map is always populated** (required for `xrealloc`'s `mem.resize` old-size correctness — NOT gating it caused a SIGSEGV in `ga_grow`/`syn_add_group`), but the expensive `backtrace()` is gated behind `NVIM_ODIN_LEAK_DEBUG=1` (`caller_if_tracked`). With tracking off, `xmalloc` is a thin `mem.alloc` + O(1) map insert; `--headless -u NONE` exits in ~0.08s and a full plugin config in ~2.3s (config-bound, not a regression). `init_memory` reads the env var via `os.lookup_env_alloc`.
- **Added EXITFREE support**: `-D CMAKE_C_FLAGS="-DEXITFREE"` in `build.sh`. C's `free_all_mem()` at exit frees persistent data, dropping tracked allocations from 8253 to near zero.
- **Fixed 2 pre-existing C leaks**: `ga_clear(&exestack)` in `free_all_mem()`, `xfree(ql_info->qf_lists)` in `free_quickfix()`.
- **Enabled Odin-side leak detection**: Tracking allocator `allocation_map` dump in atexit handler. Confirmed 0 Odin-side leaks.
- Fixed stale `CMakeCache.txt` in source tree (prevented out-of-tree builds).
- Build succeeds via `build.sh`, `--headless --clean -c "qall!"` exits cleanly with 0 leaks.
- **Ported 7 I/O primitives from `os/fs.c` Phase A to Odin**: `os_open`, `os_close`, `os_read`, `os_readv`, `os_write`, `os_fsync`, `os_open_stdin_fd`. Using `core:sys/linux` raw syscalls + `posix.errno()`/`posix.set_errno()` for errno + `uv_translate_sys_error` FFI for libuv error code conversion. All functions override C weak symbols at link time.
- **Ported `os/fs.c` Phase B to Odin + rewrote stat layer**: All filesystem symbols backed with `linux.stat`/`lstat`/`fstat` → `UVStat` (exact `uv_stat_t` mirror), fixing the latent `CStat` ABI mismatch where `FileInfo.stat` is `uv_stat_t` (uint64 fields), not raw `struct stat`. Added `os_fileinfo2` (path decomposition), `os_copy_xattr` (xattr syscalls + `emsg` FFI), `os_get_acl`/`os_set_acl`/`os_free_acl` (stubs). Switched `os_file_is_readable`/`os_file_is_writable`/`is_executable` to `linux.access` (matches C's `uv_fs_access` semantics). Deleted `src/nvim/os/fs.c` (moved to `bak/`). Verified: `getfsize`/`getfperm`/`isdirectory`/`filereadable`/`executable` all match `vim.loop.fs_stat`.
- **Fixed SIGABRT (`free(): invalid pointer`) in `os_env_exists`**: was allocating the env value with `context.temp_allocator` then `delete`-ing with the default (heap) allocator. Changed to `context.allocator` so alloc+free match. Surfaced only when the real user config called `exists('$MYVIMRC')`.
- **Fixed EPERM "operation not permitted" on `os_mkdir_recurse`**: was using `os.make_directory_all`, which opens each existing directory to walk the tree — `open()` on dirs fails with EPERM in this sandbox (while `mkdir`/`stat` are fine). Rewrote `os_mkdir_recurse` to mirror C's logic: stat-walk components, skip existing dirs (`os_isdir`), `mkdir` only missing ones via `posix.mkdir`. `os_mkdir` (single) unchanged.
- **Ported `os/fileio.c` to Odin**: All 11 functions (`file_open`, `file_open_fd`, `file_open_stdin`, `file_open_buffer`, `file_close`, `file_fsync`, `file_flush`, `file_read`, `file_try_read_buffered`, `file_write`, `file_skip`) in `src/odin/fileio.odin`. Inlined `os_file_mkdir` (uses `core:os.make_directory_all` + `filepath.dir`). Inlined buffer alloc (uses `xmalloc`/`xfree` directly instead of C `alloc_block`/`free_block` wrappers). Uses `rawptr` for pointer fields (avoids cstring pointer-math limitations in Odin).
- **Resolved Odin import conflict**: Foreign procedure `open` (libc) conflicts with `posix.open`. Solved by using `core:sys/linux` syscalls (`linux.open`, `linux.read`, `linux.write`, `linux.close`, `linux.fsync`, `linux.dup`) which have no foreign-procedure name collisions with the posix package.
- **Fixed SIGSEGV regression (alive-job exit crash)**: `Loop.children` was `Odin [dynamic]^Proc` (40 bytes: ptr+len(i32)+cap(i32)=16, plus 8-byte tail padding → 40 on this ABI) but C `kvec_t(Proc*)` is exactly 24 bytes (`size_t n; size_t a; Proc **items`). The 16-byte size mismatch shifted every Loop field AFTER `children` by 16 (e.g. `children_kill_timer` at offset 1048 in C vs 1064 in Odin), so C `proc_stop` read `children_kill_timer.loop` from the wrong address → NULL → `uv_timer_start` SIGSEGV on `jobstart(['cat'])+qa!`. Fix: replaced `[dynamic]^Proc` with an explicit `Kvec_Proc_ptr` struct (`n: c.size_t; a: c.size_t; items: ^Proc` = 24 bytes) in `src/odin/event_defs.odin`; `loop_init` zero-inits via `Kvec_Proc_ptr{}`; removed the `delete(loop.children)` in `loop_close` (C frees the kvec items). **ABI LESSON: Odin `int` is `i32` and `[dynamic]T` carries len+cap as `i32`, so `[dynamic]T` is NOT layout-compatible with a C `kvec_t`/`size_t`-based dynamic array — always mirror C containers with explicit field-for-field structs when C code reads the same memory.** Regressed gate (`jobstart(['cat'])+qa!` now exits 0, matching system nvim); 6 leak lines unchanged.
- Fixed stale `CMakeCache.txt` in source tree (prevented out-of-tree builds).

- **FIXED: full-config hang on startup (`VIMRUNTIME=runtime ./build/bin/nvim_odin` with a real config, and `system()`/`vim.system()` in any mode).** Root cause was in `src/odin/loop.odin`: `loop_poll_events` only drained `loop.fast_events`, NOT `loop.events`. `loop.events` carries every child queue (`proc->events`, `stream->s.events`, etc.) via parent links, so `proc_close_handles` (queued to `pr.events`, a child of `loop.events` by `on_proc_exit`/C `do_os_system`) was never drained during `proc_wait`'s poll loop. With no pending libuv handle left after the child exited, `uv_run(UV_RUN_ONCE)` blocked forever → `proc_wait` (`system()`) never returned → `-c` command chain / plugin bootstrap (lazy.nvim `vim.fn.system`) hung. **FIXED:** `loop_poll_events` now calls `multiqueue_process_events(loop.events)` (then `loop.fast_events`), matching C's `loop_poll_events`. Verified: `system("true")`, `system("seq 1 50000")` (288894 bytes), full LazyVim config headless `-es -c 'qa!'`, and interactive TUI `:qa!` all exit 0 with no hang. NOTE: do NOT set `proc->events`/`stream->s.events` inside `libuv_proc_spawn` — the C caller (`do_os_system`, `channel_job_start`) sets `proc->events` itself; job streams keep `s.events == nil` (synchronous) per C's `libuv_proc_spawn`/`do_os_system`.
- **Removed all `MARK` debug probes.** Odin side: `libuv_proc.odin` (`exit_cb`), `main.odin` (stdin/isatty), `proc.odin` (`proc_wait` GOT_INT), plus the now-unused `libc` imports those probes had pulled into `multiqueue.odin`/`libuv_proc.odin`/`proc.odin`. **C side: 4 probes in `src/nvim/tui/tui.c`** — `MARK tui_ui_send uv_run enter/exit` and `MARK flush_buf uv_run enter/exit` (these were printing continuously in the TUI). Because they live in C, removal required `cmake --build build --target libnvim` to rebuild `libnvim.a` before relinking the Odin binary. **LESSON: debug-probe grep must cover BOTH `src/odin/*.odin` AND `src/nvim/**/*.c` — runtime `MARK` output in the TUI came from the C static lib, not the Odin sources.** Verified via pty TUI run: screen renders, zero `MARK` in output; the legitimate `fprintf(stderr,...)` calls that remain (`main.odin` backtrace dump, `os_signal.odin` deadly-signal/invalid-signal messages, `stream.odin` "closed Stream with unwritten bytes") are upstream diagnostics, NOT probes.

- **Ported `os/pty_proc_unix.c` to Odin** (`src/odin/pty_proc.odin`): all `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols — `pty_proc_init` (returns `PtyProc` **by value**, matching C's `chan->stream.pty = pty_proc_init(...)` into the Channel union), `pty_proc_spawn`/`pty_proc_tty_name`/`pty_proc_resize`/`pty_proc_resume`/`pty_proc_flush_master`/`pty_proc_close`/`pty_proc_close_master`/`pty_proc_teardown`. Backed by `posix`/`linux` syscalls + libuv FFI; `proc.odin:106` PTY `status=-1` stub replaced with `pty_proc_spawn((^PtyProc)(pr))`. `src/nvim/os/pty_proc_unix.c` moved to `bak/os/pty_proc_unix.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. **Two teardown bugs found & fixed:** (1) `proc_close` in `proc.odin` was missing the `ProcType.Pty` → `pty_proc_close((^PtyProc)(pr))` branch — the 3rd `decref` (from `pty_proc_close`→`internal_close_cb`) that removes the child from `loop.children` never fired, so `proc_teardown`'s drain loop blocked forever (children never empties → `loop_poll_events(loop,-1)` hangs). (2) `pty_proc_init` must return `PtyProc` by value, not `^PtyProc` — a pointer return corrupts the overlapping `chan->stream.proc` union slot (`proc->type` read as garbage), which also crashed `uv_pipe_init` in `proc_spawn`. Gate + runtime pass: `jobstart(['cat'],{'pty':v:true})` spawns, `chansend`/`jobstop` work, `:terminal` pty jobs spawn/exit clean (child exits → `chld_handler`→`on_proc_exit`→`proc_close_handles`→stream closes→`proc_close`→`pty_proc_close`→`decref` → child removed from `loop.children`); `-u NONE -c qa!`, `--version`, `jobstart(['true'],{'pty':v:true})` headless `-es`, and full LazyVim config all exit 0.

- **eval.odin Batch 1+2 (2026-09-18): started `eval/` (group 7, ~28k lines) with `src/odin/eval.odin` — Batch 1: `eval/gc.c` (2 `@(export)` globals `gc_first_dict`/`gc_first_list` as rawptr + `#pragma weak` data symbols in C; nm confirms Odin B defs win over C V weaks); Batch 2: `eval/executor.c` (`eexe_mod_op` export + weak + 6 `tv_op_*_o` plain `proc "c"` statics). Reused `Typval_T` (option.odin), `Garray` (main.odin), `ga_grow_r`/`vim_strchr_c` (fold.odin), `concat_str_c` (option.odin), `smsg`/`semsg` #c_vararg (shell.odin), `NUMBUFLEN` (digraph.odin); new FFI only for `tv_get_number`/`tv_get_string`/`tv_get_string_buf`/`tv_list_extend`/`grow_string_tv`/`tv_clear`/`num_divide`/`num_modulus` (sigs from `eval/typval.h.generated.h`); `tv_blob_len`/`tv_list_ref` are C static inlines → offset reimpls (bv_ga.ga_len@blob+0, lv_refcount@list+56, all cc-probed); e_letwrong is EXTERN array → Odin literal "E734…" (array gotcha). CRITICAL BUG found by gdb (SEGV at first `let n += 3`): `Typval_T.vval` is INLINE union storage, not a pointer — `(^C.longlong)(tv.vval)^ = n` writes to address 0x7. Reads AND writes must transmute (`transmute(C.longlong)(tv.vval)`, `tv.vval = transmute(rawptr)(n)`; same for f64/pointers) — window.odin:7859 write precedent, no read precedent existed. LESSON: for `[N]u8`/rawptr union stand-ins, the field VALUE is the payload — never dereference it (same class as slot-address reads, but inverted: there the address held the pointer, here the field IS the data). VERIFY: 20-case operator probe (number/float/string/list/blob/div/mod + 6 E734 paths + null-coercion) byte-identical to ref; unone/lazy/buffer gates exit 0.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-25): LazyVim TUI abort on startup / f-press (user report) — ROOT CAUSE: my `call_user_func` (Batch 24s) called `estack_push_ufunc_e(fp, 1)` but NEVER popped — C does `estack_pop()` before restoring sctx. Every user-function call leaked one exestack entry, leaving stale UFUNC frames on top; the next autocmd iteration's `aucmd_next` (which reads top-of-stack blindly) then treated a dead function frame as its iteration entry and XFREE_CLEAR'd the function name → double-free (`free(): invalid pointer`). DEBUG CHAIN: tmux-pty repro → ASAN double-free with full stacks → minimized to `syntax on` + edit (no plugins needed!) → gdb SET/CLEAR trace showed CLEARs at depths with no matching SET → conditional breakpoint on top-type != AUCMD caught UFUNC frames → found push-without-pop by comparing against bak/userfunc.c. One-line fix: `estack_pop_r()` before sctx restore (C order). LESSON: symmetric C push/pop pairs must BOTH be ported — grep every ported push for its pop. VERIFY: minimal repro + ASAN clean + 5× f+o LazyVim TUI cycles alive + exit 0 + full probe/gate battery green.**

REAL BUG (mine, probe-caught): initialized `ret: C.int = 0` then guarded the body with `if ret != FAIL_E` — but `FAIL_E == 0`, so the `call_func` body never ran and every `custom,Func` completion returned nil (direct `Func()` calls worked since they bypass this wrapper; rename-control build proved C impls run the callback). Restructured to C-faithful `fail`-flag (v:lua arm sets fail, body runs on `!fail`). LESSON: never guard on a sentinel-valued local you just initialized to that sentinel — C's uninitialized-`ret` + `goto fail` pattern must become an explicit bool flag. - **ex_docmd.odin Batch 23e (2026-09-27): cmdline engine LIVE — 1 export + weak (`do_cmdline`, ~600 lines; activates all dormant 23c/23d plains) + 7 plains (`get/store_loop_line`, `msg_verbose_cmd`, `save/restore_dbg_stuff_o`, `sourcing_name`, `deep_clear_lines_ga_o`) + moved `do_cmdline_recursive` file-global.** dbg_stuff offsets cc-probed (64B); DOCMD_EXCRESET/REPEAT/KEEPLINE (headers); cstack/wcmd/ga/exestack/Estack mirrors reused. ENTIRE Ex path now Odin (do_cmdline→do_one_cmd_o→parse→execute). REAL BUGS ×2 (both mine, probe+gdb-found): (1) argopt-fail reused the single-pass `finished` flag (set at loop top) as its goto flag → `if finished break` fired UNCONDITIONALLY for every EX_ARGOPT command (`:edit`/`:read`/`:write` silently did nothing; `:cd`/`:qa!` unaffected) — dedicated `argopt_failed` flag. (2) `ea.cmdlinep` stored the STRING not the POINTER (C stores `char**`) → repl_cmdline wrote the new buffer into ea's field + freed the shared buffer → `free(): invalid pointer` on `edit #` (gdb: xfree("edit #") ← do_cmdline). Then a level-confusion fix: execute_cmd0_o passed &field instead of field value → E41 OOM. LESSONS: `char**` out-params must store the pointer, and every level of indirection needs explicit tracking; single-pass-for + shared done-flag is unsound — one flag per goto-site. TECH DEBT: ~130-line /* DUP-SPAN */ commented duplicate inside do_one_cmd_o (harmless, builds clean — remove when convenient). VERIFY: full 10-probe re-sweep 10/10 + unone/lazy green. NEXT: Batch 24 (`do_exmode`? final audit → CUTOVER?).**

- **FIXED (2026-09-29): `c` → SIGILL in `--embed` server, outer exits 132 (user report).** GDB caught it: Odin `bounds_trap` (`index=-3, count=4`) in my `may_trigger_modechanged` pattern join (`state.odin`) — I indexed `curr_mode[pp - 5]` reusing the old-mode cursor instead of a fresh index (always negative for short modes → trap on the FIRST mode change with a ModeChanged autocmd defined, e.g. via hit-enter prompt → cmdline). FIX: separate `j` index for the new mode. WHY IT HID: needs a ModeChanged autocmd (absent in `-u NONE`, so all gate/probe batteries passed) + a real mode change; `-u NONE` TUI tests never define the autocmd. LESSONS: (1) never reuse a cursor across two buffers — one index per array, always; (2) `ulimit -c` is 0 here so cores never materialize (use the abort-hook build when a core is needed); (3) verify `:autocmd`-gated paths with the autocmd DEFINED (add ModeChanged-battery to the standard sweep). ROOT CAUSE: `va_list` crossed the C→Odin FFI **by value** (my `logmsg_v`/`v_do` took `va: libc.va_list` struct) but C array params decay — the shim pushes an 8-byte POINTER while Odin read a 24-byte struct → `vfprintf` walked garbage. - **FIXED (2026-09-28): user-reported `E10` on LazyVim startup (`:xnoremap` with `\|` in matchit `MatchEnable`). ROOT CAUSE: my Batch-5 `separate_nextcmd` flattened C's `for (; *p; MB_PTR_ADV(p))` into advance-only-in-else — after backslash-removal (and in the Ctrl-V/backtick arms) my loop re-examined the current char instead of advancing past it. For `\|\|`, C removes `\` then SKIPS the bar; mine re-examined it with a non-`\` predecessor → split → `nextcmd=\| col...` → second do_one_cmd → get_address E10. DEBUG: staged-fprintf bisection (DBGGETADDR → DBGPARSECMD showed the phantom second parse). FIX: bottom-of-loop `MB_PTR_ADV` advance on every non-break path (exact C mirror). LESSON: C `for(init;cond;incr)` ports must preserve the increment on ALL paths, not just the else — audit every flattened loop. VERIFY: repro silent, map-RHS byte-identical (`\|`→`|`), bar/comment battery + gates + focused re-runs green; probes removed.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-26): lazy-startup `free(): invalid pointer` (user-visible gate regression from 28d) — ROOT CAUSE: latent upstream UB that C survives by stack luck. C\u2019s ex_while declares bare `evalarg_T evalarg;`, fill sets only flags/getline/cookie, and NOTHING in the tree ever stores non-NULL eval_tofree (proven by exhaustive grep — the bak/userfunc setter is dead). So clear_evalarg gambles on uninitialized stack: C\u2019s residue happened NULL, mine was 0xcfd9 → swap poisoned *cmdlinep → do_one_cmd xfree(stack) → abort. DEBUG CHAIN: gate failure → gdb (xfree←do_one_cmd) → offsets re-probed with REAL build defines (identical — exonerated) → temporary fprintf probe (values untrusted? no — #c_vararg proven) → gdb conditional breakpoint caught ex_while :for path with tofree=garbage + *cmdlinep==cmdlinep-slot → grep proved zero setters. FIX: zero eval_tofree in fill (every clear is preceded by fill by API contract; no legitimate behavior exists to preserve). LESSON: uninitialized-stack gambles that pass in C can fail in Odin (different frames/residue) — when porting, initialize what C leaves indeterminate if no writer exists. VERIFY: lazy gate 0, probes clean, all debug removed.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-25): pre-existing heap corruption in os_expand_wildcards + latent cursor bugs in expand_env_esc (surfaced by 27t `${HOME}` probe: every both-brace input lost exactly its last 2 chars).** DEBUG CHAIN: dismissed NUL-length/split-brain/expand_T-size (assert 424 ✓)/mirror-offset theories; entry-trace showed env code never runs for these inputs → has_special_wildchar routes `{` to os_expand_wildcards → exit-trace proved MY function returns the truncated string → root cause in post-filter copy: `cstr_len(ptr+1)` twice where C has `strlen(ptr)` (NUL `+1` misplaced INTO the pointer: 7B buffer + 6-limit copy = 5-char result). ALSO fixed: command `len` used strlen(p_sh) instead of strlen(tempname) (C bak/shell.c; buffer underallocation → heap overflow), and two dst-vs-cursor bugs in expand_env_esc (C advances `dst` as cursor; mine froze it: vim_getenv(dst)→(d), os_get_userdir(var+1)→(d+1)). Rename-control build exonerated f_expand (C version corrupts identically — shared machinery). LESSON: when C reuses a param as cursor, mirror with explicit cursor local and audit EVERY use of the original name. VERIFY: full brace battery + expandcmd + system/glob + all gates green; all DBG probes removed.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-23): rare heap abort/SEGV (user's f+ESC crashes) — ROOT CAUSE: wrong offset constant in my `did_set_spelloptions` (optionstr.odin, Batch 9): used buffer-relative `SB_P_SPO_FLAGS` (= B_S_OFF+1128 = 12392) on a *synblock* pointer instead of synblock-relative `SB_P_SPO_FLAGS_OFF` (= 1128), writing 4 bytes 11264 past the field into adjacent/freed heap. C computes `&win->w_s->b_p_spo_flags`. DEBUG CHAIN: flaky boot abort → strace SIGCHLD red herring → cores all aborting in `ts_query_new` free (detector, not cause) → Odin heap IS libc (heap_allocator_unix calls malloc/free) → ASAN build (had to drop `-lasan`, Odin's `-sanitize:address` brings its own runtime; CFLAGS ASAN impossible — poisons host generators) → ASAN nailed heap-UAF WRITE at optionstr.odin:41 on iter0, 12/12 hits, same low address bits → traced to window-local flags write → constant audit found it. LESSON: dual offset families (`B_S_OFF+X` for buffers vs bare `X_OFF` for synblocks) must never cross; audit all `SB_P_*` uses (only this site was wrong). VERIFY: spelloptions set/append/clear/invalid byte-identical to ref; ASAN loop no longer reports the spo UAF; unone/lazy gates 0; 400× f+ESC TUI + 10 native boots + 6 MALLOC_PERTURB_ boots all clean. All debug probes removed; clean rebuild. - **AUDIT (2026-09-23): event/stream callback calling conventions — ROOT CAUSE of the ASAN `read_event` face: plain Odin procs take a hidden `context` param (`main::read_event` disasm: `rdi=argv, rsi=context`); the only C-direct event invocation (`state.c:118 event.handler(event.argv)`) passes argv correctly but leaves `rsi` garbage, so the handler's first line (`context = runtime.default_context()`) writes ~200B through a wild pointer — usually benign slack corruption, occasionally fatal (matches abort/SEGV duality + rarity + f+ESC pumping via `state_handle_k_event`). Same hazard in all libuv-facing plain procs. FIX: converted the entire callback surface to `proc "c"` (safe under both callers): `argv_callback` typedef + 12 handlers (read/cursorhold/loop_deferred/oneshot/proc_close(_handles)/exit/signal/connection/time/close/out_data), typedefs (`signal/time/stream_read-write-close/socket/proc_exit-state-internal_cb`) + fillers (`on_signal`, `deadly_signal`, `on_proc_exit`, `decref`, `on_proc_stream_close`, `rstream_close_cb`, `connect_close_cb`, `shell_write_cb`, `out_data_cb`, `input_read_cb`, `system_data_cb`), libuv shims (`exit_cb`, `libuv_proc_close_cb`, `children_kill_cb`, `loop_walk/async/timer(_close)_cb`, `exit_delay_cb`, `signal_watcher_cb`), helpers (`event_create`, `kv_children_*`, `flush_stream`, `proc_close`), asserts→if+abort where `assert` lost its context. Compiler-enumerated every site; `PutCallback`/`wbuffer_data_finalizer`/C-function fillers verified safe (Odin-invoked or context-ignoring). `loop_on_put` stays exported-plain (address-taken by C, invoked only from `"c"` multiqueue_put_event). VERIFY: ASAN loop 12/12 clean (was 12/12 crashing at `read_event`); timer/job/system/arity probe byte-identical to ref; unone/lazy gates 0.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-22): user-reported f+ESC TUI crash (abort or SIGSEGV) — ROOT CAUSE: `highlight_attr_g: [^]C.int` foreign bind on C ARRAY `int highlight_attr[]` loads the array's first 8 bytes AS the pointer (gdb: base=0x1000000000 = attr[0..1]). Any spell-highlight redraw (`set spell` + redraw) segfaulted in `win_line` (drawline.odin:1678). Reproduced deterministically headless (`set spell` + edit + `redraw!` → 139), fixed by declaring `[HLF_COUNT_O]C.int` (bounds-checked). SAME-CLASS FIX: `eval_msgpack_type_lists_e: [^]rawptr` → `[8]rawptr` (was loading first element NULL as base). RULE: C `T[]` symbols bind as `[N]T`, never `[^]T`/`rawptr` (those load element bytes as address); C `T*` symbols bind as `[^]T` (loads the pointer — correct; verified `hl_attr_active`, `linebuf_*`). Audited all foreign `[^]` vars. VERIFY: spell redraw exit 0; spellbadword/suggest + dup-key special-dict + msgpack roundtrip + json_encode(special) byte-identical to ref; unone/lazy gates 0. OPEN: separate flaky boot-time SIGSEGV (strace: SIGCHLD after `git --version` child exits → SEGV at 0x7d00000098, no keys pressed) — needs its own repro.**

- **FIXED (2026-09-19): abort in picker scrolling (`utfc_next_impl: Assertion *next >= 0x80`, user report).** Backtrace: `utfc_next_impl` ← OUR `win_line` (drawline.odin:852) ← `win_update` — hit while rendering scrolled lines with multibyte icons. ROOT CAUSE: C calls the ASCII-guarded wrapper `utfc_next()` (mbyte.h:93: peek next byte, inline-advance if ASCII, else impl), but our port called `utfc_next_impl_r` directly, whose assert requires a multibyte lead. One-line-class fix: added `utfc_next_o` wrapper (mirrors mbyte.h:93 exactly) + rewired the single call site (only one in package). LESSON: header-inline wrappers with preconditions (`_impl` suffix) must be ported as wrappers, never bypassed — grep for `_impl` FFI decls when a crash lands in one. VERIFY: build clean, unone/lazy/order-which-key gates green; picker scroll death-probe no longer hits this assert.
- **NOT-OURS (2026-09-19): which-key popup shows `Bar` instead of `|` (user: "wrong place" — actually a wider popup shifting centered col 61 vs 63).** ROOT CAUSE: upstream vim-patch 9.2.0680 (in-tree since June 2026, `keytrans() doesn't replace '|' and '\'`) changed `keytrans("|")` from `|` to `<Bar>`; this which-key version formats `<Bar>` → `Bar`. Stock is v0.12.5 (predates the patch), hence the diff. The whole path is unported C (`f_keytrans` → `str2special_save`; only garray helpers are ours and output is correct). A pristine C build of this tree would show the identical `Bar`. No port action; plugin needs a newer release.
- **FIXED (2026-09-19): which-key help footer (`esc close / back / scroll` line) rendered outside the popup at screen (21,0) instead of its last row (user's actual "out of place" report).** Grid-dump proof: stock renders it inside at row 27, ours detached at col 2. ROOT CAUSE: `ui_ext_win_position` (window.odin, Batch 46) read `relative`/`bufpos.lnum` with win_T-ABSOLUTE consts (`WCFG_RELATIVE_OFF=10604`, `WCFG_BUFPOS_LNUM_OFF=10564) added to a WinConfig-RELATIVE `wcfg` base — wild memory ≠ `KFLOAT_REL_WINDOW`, so the anchor branch was skipped and raw config (21,0) used unanchored. Same absolute-vs-relative class as Batch 38 (which only fixed `style`/`cmdline`). FIX: new `WCFG_REL_RELATIVE_OFF=44` / `WCFG_REL_BUFPOS_LNUM_OFF=4` (cc-probed WinConfig layout) + rewired 4 use sites (relative×3 incl. laststatus/tabline arms, bufpos.lnum×2); absolute consts stay for wp-based walks (win_comp_pos, win_alloc). VERIFY: footer pos (27,62) vs stock (27,64) — delta is exactly the known keytrans width drift; rel=win anchoring probe (`row=9,col=0` on popup at (5,60) → (14,60)) byte-identical to stock; unone/lazy/window gates exit 0. LESSON: every `wcfg + OFFSET` site must state its frame — audit all WinConfig-relative readers whenever adding an absolute one and vice versa.**

### Known Issues
- **OPEN (2026-09-19): intermittent memline read fault during snacks-picker treesitter preview (`ml_get_buf_len` SEGV / `ml_line_textlen > 0` assert; user: errors/abort while typing in find-file).** Backtrace: `input_cb` (lua/treesitter.c) ← lua external scanner ← `ts_parser_parse` ← `parser_parse` ← `nlua_schedule_event` (vim.schedule) ← `state_handle_k_event`. So a SCHEDULED parse reads garbage block data for a LIVE buffer (buflist-verified, sane `ml_line_count`, no wipe ever observed, content recently replaced 371→805 lines). Evidence: pre-order binary (old loop.events-draining polls) 3/3 clean, new code ~1/4 clean, stock 0.12.5 3/3 clean, eval ports exonerated (C-eval binary still dies), undo-disabled still dies, ASAN clean 3/3 (timing-shifted, inconclusive), tight set_lines churn probes (900/5000-line, undo+marks) clean. Ruled out: stale `ml_line` cache-hit (MLDBG probe, zero hits), misdirected `ml_line_count` writes (audited), missing queue drains (all `multiqueue_process_events`/`loop_poll_events`/`inbuf_poll` sites verified against C incl. `loop_uv_run` modes and `os_input_ready`), klib put/del/rehash by inspection. Leading theory: stale scheduled parse vs content-replacement timing (old eager draining masked it; faithful batching exposes it); needs upstream-0.13-C comparison to tell port-bug from upstream race. Repro: `/tmp/opencode/pkdeath_rpc.py` (embed + LazyVim + `f` + type/nav/backspace loops).
- **Timers do not fire while blocked waiting for typeahead** (pre-existing input/event-loop gap): `feedkeys('...', 'xt')` that blocks on incomplete input never runs `timer_start` callbacks (C nvim does). Repro: `timer_start(50,{->feedkeys(",bar<Esc>q",'t')}) + feedkeys('qrct[','xt!')` completes on `/usr/bin/nvim`, hangs on ours. Suspect `inbuf_poll`'s blocking wait doesn't process timer events (or stdin-EOF handling differs). Breaks oldtests using the timer-feedkeys pattern (e.g. `Test_replay_charsearch_omap`).
- **15 small C allocations (~776 B total) remain unfreed when file is opened** — temporary strings from `cstr_to_string`, `do_cmdline`, etc. Pre-existing C infrastructure leaks not cleaned by `free_all_mem()`. Not from Odin port.
- `mf_hash` uses `old_new` as block number which collides with new allocations before they are committed in `mf_put`. A `pmap_del` then removes the block from the hash if its bnum was `old_new`, even though the block at `old_new` is actually a different one.
- **Signal-triggered exit (e.g. SIGTERM/SIGINT, `SIGHUP`) to the TUI, AND a keypress during normal TUI use, segfaulted** in `unpacker_advance`→`mpack_parse_tok` with a corrupted `channel->rpc.unpacker` (garbage `parser.size`/`parser.status`, crash at `parent->pos`). **Root cause was a heap buffer overflow in `src/odin/rstream.odin`:** `rstream_space` was computed against `read_pos` instead of `write_pos` (upstream uses `write_pos`). With the wrong formula, `alloc_cb` handed libuv a write buffer extending PAST the 4096-byte `RSTREAM_BLOCK_SIZE` allocation, so incoming bytes overflowed into the next heap chunk — which happened to be the `Unpacker` struct (its leading `parser` field got clobbered → garbage `parser.size`). The overflow also manifested on the first keypress/redraw because that's when the read buffer first fills. **FIXED:** `rstream_space` now uses `write_pos` (matching upstream rstream.c), so `alloc_cb`'s `buf.len` is correct and the existing `rstream_invoke_read_cb` pause logic (`if !rstream_space { rstream_stop_inner; paused_full=true }`) prevents writes past the buffer end. Also removed a stray `fprintf(stderr,"closing Stream ...")` debug print left in `read_cb` (upstream uses `DLOG`). Additionally hardened the channel lifetime: `Stream.data_incref`/`data_decref` callbacks set by `rpc_start` (msgpack_rpc/channel.c) on the read stream, bumped in `rstream_invoke_read_cb`/`read_event` so a queued `read_event` keeps its channel alive until run; `free_channel_event` skips+reschedules when `refcount > 0`; `receive_msgpack` bails if `rpc.closed`. Verified: 120× `SIGHUP`, 60× normal keypress/edit TUI sessions, `:qa!`, `--version` all exit 0 with no crash; debug instrumentation fully removed.
- **Teardown of a `--listen` server emits a benign `'event' item` validation emsg and exits 1 (not a crash — no SIGSEGV).** Reproduced: system nvim ALSO exits 1 when a `--listen` server is quit via client `qa!` ("Caught deadly signal" path); our port additionally prints `'event' item` from `nvim_get_autocmds` (`api/autocmd.c:136`) during `free_all_mem`'s autocmd teardown. Likely tied to the pre-existing `mf_hash`/`syntaxset` corruption (Known Issues above) corrupting an augroup's event field, exposed because the socket server's teardown walks autocmds. Non-functional — RPC and listen/connect work correctly. Low priority.
- ~~**LazyVim full config SIGSEGV in C `match_suffix` reading a garbage `fname` from the `files` array**~~ — **ROOT CAUSED & FIXED (2026-07).** The crash was NOT pre-existing heap corruption. The real bug: in `src/odin/shell.odin`'s `os_expand_wildcards` realloc/fill loop, `j` was declared as a `for j, i := c.int(0), c.int(0); ...` **loop variable**. In Odin, loop-variable lifetime ends at loop exit, so `num_file^ = j` (read AFTER the loop) pulled **garbage** (29) instead of the true count (1). The `file` array was allocated for 1 slot but `*num_file` was set to 29, so C's `expand_wildcards` iterated `match_suffix((*files)[i])` out-of-bounds → `heap-buffer-overflow` → SIGSEGV. **Fix:** declared `j` (and `i`) OUTSIDE the loop so their storage persists past loop exit; `num_file^ = j` now writes the correct count. Verified with ASAN: LazyVim `-u ~/.config/nvim/init.lua --headless -c 'qa!'` exits 0, no overflow. **LESSON: never read a `for`-loop variable after the loop in Odin** — declare the counter outside, or capture it into a separate variable before loop exit. All `-u NONE` shell ops also pass: `system()`, `system("seq 1 50000")`, `glob()`, `getcmdoutput()`, `:!`, `:r !`, `:w !`, escaping.

 8. **Port `event/` (libuv event loop) to Odin** — keep libuv engine (FFI via `-luv`); reimplement nvim-specific logic. ✅ Done: `event/multiqueue.c` → `src/odin/multiqueue.odin` + `src/odin/event_defs.odin` AND `event/loop.c` → `src/odin/loop.odin` + `src/odin/uv_defs.odin`. **Also done:** `event/signal.c` → `src/odin/signal.odin`; `os/signal.c` → `src/odin/os_signal.odin`; `os/time.c` → `bak/os_time.c` (funcs now in `time.odin`); `event/stream.c` → `src/odin/stream.odin`; `event/rstream.c` → `src/odin/rstream.odin`; `event/wstream.c` → `src/odin/wstream.odin`; **`event/socket.c` → `src/odin/socket.odin`** (all 8+ `@(export)` symbols: `socket_watcher_init`/`start`/`accept`/`close`, `socket_connect`, `socket_address_tcp_host_end`, `connection_cb`/`connection_event` — C `event/socket.c` moved to `bak/event/socket.c`). Gate + runtime pass: TCP **and** unix-socket servers listen on the correct port; `nvim --listen` accepts connections and serves RPC in BOTH directions (our-server↔system-client and our-client↔our-server both return correct eval results for TCP and pipe transports); `jobstart`, `timer_start`, signals, and `--headless -c qall!` all exit clean. **Gotchas:** (a) `uv_signal_t.signum` offset is **104**. (b) `on_signal` is Odin-calling-convention (called from `signal_event`), not `proc "c"`. (c) `signal_name` returns `transmute(^u8)(cstring(...))`. (d) `preserve_exit` FFI takes `cstring` → pass `cstring(&IObuff[0])`. (e) `ml_sync_all` 3rd arg is `bool` → `true`. (f) **`Loop.children: [dynamic]^Proc` is NOT ABI-compatible with C `kvec_t(Proc*)`** — use explicit 24-byte `Kvec_Proc_ptr` struct (was causing SIGSEGV at `proc_stop` on alive-job exit). (g) **Job-pipe stdout requires `stream.s.events` wired to `proc->events`.** C's `stream_init` always sets `stream->events = NULL`; the C job path (`libuv_proc_spawn` in `proc.c`) now sets `proc->out.s.events = proc->events;` and `proc->err.s.events = proc->events;` after their `stream_init(NULL, ...)` calls (the FD/terminal paths set `events = loop.events` inside `stream_init` because `loop != nil`). Without this, `rstream_invoke_read_cb` would call `multiqueue_put_event(nil, ...)` and assert-crash; with it, job stdout is delivered and the job tears down cleanly. (h) `RStream.buffer`/`read_pos`/`write_pos` are `uintptr` in Odin (not `^u8`) to permit `uintptr` pointer arithmetic (`^u8 + c.size_t` is a type mismatch). `uv_handle_t`/`uv_stream_t` in `uv_defs.odin` carry a `type: c.int` at offset 16 inside the existing `data`/`loop`/`pad` layout (sizes preserved: handle 96, stream 248). (i) `read_event`/`rstream_invoke_read_cb` are Odin-calling-convention procs (called from `multiqueue`/`read_cb`), not `proc "c"`. (j) **`CREATE_EVENT` nil-queue semantics**: C's `CREATE_EVENT(q, h, ...)` calls `h(argv)` directly when `q == NULL`. Odin ports that did `multiqueue_put_event(watcher.events, e)` unconditionally ASSERT-crashed (nil queue) for server sockets whose `events` stays NULL. Fix: added `create_event(queue, event)` in `multiqueue.odin` that mirrors `CREATE_EVENT` (nil → direct `event.handler(&event.argv[0])`). Used by `socket.odin`, `rstream.odin`, `signal.odin`, `time_watcher.odin`. (k) **libuv struct offsets must be verified against the system libuv, not assumed**: `uv_getaddrinfo_t.addrinfo` is at offset **144** (not 72/80) — real layout is `uv_req_t(64) + loop(8) + getaddrinfo_cb(8) + 64 pad + addrinfo(8)`; `uv_connect_t.handle` is at offset **72** (cb at 64) — my first attempt had `handle` at 64 (missing 8 bytes of `uv_req_t`), causing `connect_cb` to read a garbage `handle->data` → SIGSEGV on connection error. Verify every `uv_*` struct offset with a tiny C program (`__builtin_offsetof`) before trusting it. (l) **`_try_getdigits` advances its `**char` argument**: C's `try_getdigits(&(char*){port}, ...)` advances a TEMPORARY, leaving the original `port` intact; my Odin `_try_getdigits(&port, ...)` advanced `port` itself past the digits, so the subsequent `if (*port == NUL) port = NULL` nulled the port and `uv_getaddrinfo` got no service → server bound to a RANDOM port. Fix: parse into a temp (`tmp := port; _try_getdigits(&tmp, &iport)`), keep `port` pointing at the service string. (m) `socket_address_tcp_host_end` is `proc "c"` (called from C `main.c`/`server.c`); the `SocketWatcher`/`addrinfo`/`uv_*` struct layouts in `event_defs.odin`/`uv_defs.odin` are byte-for-byte compatible with C's `src/nvim/event/defs.h`. **Next (dependency order, bottom-up):** re-port `os/input.c` → `libuv_proc.odin`+`pty_proc_unix.odin` → `proc.odin` → re-port `os/shell.c`. Then the ~20 C consumers (channel.c, terminal.c, tui, eval.c, msgpack_rpc, etc.) keep linking via the kept C `.h` prototypes. Win-only event files excluded from Linux build.
 9. ~~**Port `os/signal.c` to Odin**~~ — ✅ Done: fully covered by `src/odin/os_signal.odin`; C source moved to `bak/os_signal.c`. (Note: `src/odin/signal.odin` is the distinct port of `src/nvim/event/signal.c`, also done.)
   10. **Port remaining `os/` event-coupled files onto the Odin event loop** — ✅ **ALL DONE**. `os/shell.c` (see DONE note), `os/input.c` (see DONE note above), `os/pty_proc_unix.c`, `os/signal.c`, `os/lang.c`, `os/env.c` (A+B), `os/proc.c`, `os/fs.c` (A+B), `os/fileio.c`, `os/time.c`, `os/dl.c`. **The non-Windows `os/` layer is fully ported to Odin.** Each C source moved to `bak/`. **CRITICAL CORRECTION (verified 2026-07):** None of these were "partially started" — every Odin reference to their symbols was a pure `---` `foreign _` forward declaration (symbol reservation linking to C); the C files provided 100% of the real logic until cutover. (`pty_proc_*`/`shell_*`/`input_*` are now real `@(export)` procs in their `.odin` files.) Remaining C-only reservations that still resolve to C symbols: `get_cmd_output`→`os_lang.odin` (now FFI-reserved onto C's shell.c-compat symbol), `shell_free_argv`→`proc.odin`, plus the msg/screen/UI helpers listed in each port's FFI block.
  - **PTY DONE (verified 2026-07)**: `os/pty_proc_unix.c` → `src/odin/pty_proc.odin` (all `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols: `pty_proc_init`/`pty_proc_spawn`/`pty_proc_tty_name`/`pty_proc_resize`/`pty_proc_resume`/`pty_proc_flush_master`/`pty_proc_close`/`pty_proc_close_master`/`pty_proc_teardown`/`pty_proc_init`). Backed by `posix`/`linux` syscalls + libuv FFI; `proc.odin:106` PTY stub replaced with `pty_proc_spawn((^PtyProc)(pr))`; `src/nvim/os/pty_proc_unix.c` moved to `bak/os/pty_proc_unix.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. Gate + runtime pass: `jobstart(['cat'],{'pty':v:true})` spawns, `chansend`/`jobstop` work, `:terminal` pty jobs spawn/exit clean (child exits → `chld_handler`→`on_proc_exit`→`proc_close_handles`→stream closes→`proc_close`→`pty_proc_close`→`internal_close_cb`(decref) → child removed from `loop.children`). **Gotchas (this Odin build, dev-2026-07):** (a) `pty_proc_init` MUST return `PtyProc` **by value** (matching C's `chan->stream.pty = pty_proc_init(...)` into a `union { Proc proc; PtyProc pty; ... }` — NOT a `^PtyProc` pointer, which corrupts the overlapping `chan->stream.proc` slot). Same for `libuv_proc_init` (already by value). (b) `proc_close` in `proc.odin` MUST handle `ProcType.Pty` → `pty_proc_close((^PtyProc)(pr))` (the 3rd `decref` that removes the child from `loop.children` comes from `pty_proc_close`→`internal_close_cb`); missing this branch hangs `proc_teardown` forever (children never empties). (c) `posix`/`linux` fd type friction: use `posix.FD`/`posix.open`/`posix.fcntl` (returns `FD`, not a tuple); `linux.ioctl`/`linux.close` take `linux.Fd` (cast `linux.Fd(fd)`); bit_set flags need NAMED bits (`posix.O_Flags{.RDWR, .NOCTTY}`, `posix.FCNTL_Cmd.SETFL`, `posix.Wait_Flags{.NOHANG}`, `posix.Poll_Event{.IN}`, `posix.Signal(SIGKILL)`, `posix.Errno.EINTR`) — integer casts to bit_set/enum fail. (d) `posix.signal`/`kill`/`killpg` take `Signal`/`pid_t` enum types (cast c.int constants). (e) `environ` is already declared `@(link_name="environ")` as `_c_environ: ^cstring` in `os_env_b.odin`; assign `(_c_environ = (^cstring)(child_environ))` in the forked child. (f) `uv_signal_start`/`uv_signal_stop`/`uv_pipe_open`/`uv_chdir` are already declared in `uv_defs.odin` — do NOT redeclare in `pty_proc.odin`.
  - **FIXED: `:terminal cat` (a live pty child at quit) hung forever on `qa!`** (`terminal-cat-headless`/`terminal-cat-tui` were exit 137; `terminal true`/`jobstop`/`jobstart` all worked). **Root cause: the Odin `Stream` struct field order in `src/odin/event_defs.odin` did NOT match C's `struct stream` in `src/nvim/event/defs.h`.** Odin had `data_incref`/`data_decref` placed right after `cb_data` (near the top), but C declares them at the very END (after `write_cb`/`curmem`/`maxmem`). Because C code reads `Stream` fields through the shared `Channel.stream` union, this shifted `pending_reqs` (and everything after it) to the wrong offset. C's `term_delayed_free` (`channel.c:948`) checks `chan->stream.proc.in.pending_reqs || chan->stream.proc.out.s.pending_reqs` and requeues itself to `chan->events` while non-zero — reading a wrong (garbage, non-zero) offset made it requeue forever, so `multiqueue_process_events(loop.events)` spun infinitely (never returning to `uv_run`), and `proc_teardown`'s drain never completed. **Fix:** reordered Odin `Stream` to be byte-for-byte identical to C — `data_incref`/`data_decref` moved to the end (after `maxmem`). **DEBUG METHOD that found it:** probes showed `proc_close_streams` reported `in{closed=1 preq=0} out{closed=1 preq=0}` (streams properly closed, reqs zero) yet a spin-counter in `multiqueue_process_events` hit 100000s of iterations on one handler; `nm` resolved that handler address to `term_delayed_free` — proving C read a *different* `pending_reqs` than Odin wrote → struct layout mismatch. **LESSON: ANY Odin struct that C reads through a union/shared pointer must match the C field order EXACTLY, field-for-field — a mis-ordered field compiles and links fine but silently corrupts every field after it.** Verified: `terminal cat` (headless + TUI pty via `script`), `terminal true`, `pty jobstop`, `jobstart`, `system()`/`system("seq 1 50000")`, RPC TCP roundtrip (`rpcrequest nvim_eval '1+2'` → 3), and full LazyVim config `-es -c qa!` all exit 0. NOTE: `proc_teardown`'s drain mirrors C's `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS`/`LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS_UNTIL` (`multiqueue.h:26-52`) — only call `loop_poll_events(loop,-1)` when `loop.events` is EMPTY; when it has events, `multiqueue_process_events` them WITHOUT polling (a bare poll after every process step deadlocks on requeueing handlers). `chld_handler` (SIGCHLD via `children_watcher`) never even fired in this path — the pty child's exit is driven entirely through the stream-close → `decref` chain, not signal reaping.
   - **DONE (verified 2026-07): Port `os/shell.c` (1349 lines) → `src/odin/shell.odin`** — all `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols: `os_expand_wildcards`, `shell_build_argv`, `shell_free_argv`, `shell_argv_to_str`, `os_call_shell`, `call_shell`, `os_system`, `get_cmd_output`. Backed by the already-ported process machinery (`libuv_proc_init`/`proc_spawn`/`proc_wait`/`rstream_*`/`wstream_*`/`multiqueue_*`/`loop_poll_events`) + `foreign _` FFI to C-only helpers (msg/screen/UI/misc). `StringBuilder` mirrored as `{size,capacity,items:^u8}` with `kv_*` helpers. `src/nvim/os/shell.c` moved to `bak/os/shell.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. **Verify BEFORE cutover (all passed):** `system("echo hello")`, `system("seq 1 50000")` (288894 bytes captured), `:!ls`, `:r !cmd` (read filter inserts file), `:w !cmd` (write filter, `wc -l`→2), `expand()`/glob (`os_expand_wildcards`), `getcmdoutput()`, shell escaping (`:!echo 'a b'`), `--version`, `-u NONE -c qa!`. **Gotchas (this Odin build, dev-2026-07):** (a) Pointer deref is POSTFIX `^` (`file: ^^^u8` → `file^`), not C `*file`. (b) `^^u8` (array of `^u8`) vs `^u8` (byte buffer) need distinct casts: `([^]^u8)(pat)[i]` for the string array, `([^]u8)(buffer)[i]` for the byte buffer; bare `pa0[_l]` fails → `([^]u8)(pa0)[_l]`. (c) No pointer arithmetic — `(^u8)(uintptr(p) + n)`; compare via `uintptr(p) < uintptr(end)`. (d) `for i := 0` invalid → `for i := c.int(0)`. (e) `check_secure()` returns `bool`; `secure` is `bool`; `no_wait_return` is a package-global `bool` in main.odin (use `= true`, not `+= 1`). (f) `os_strerror` C macro = `uv_strerror`; FFI'd as `@(link_name="uv_strerror")`. (g) `core:c/libc` provides `fopen/fclose/fseek/ftell/fread` with `^libc.FILE`, `libc.long`, `libc.Whence{SET,END,CUR}`. (h) `OUT_DATA_THRESHOLD :: 1024*10` / `MAX_CHUNK_SIZE :: OUT_DATA_THRESHOLD/2` MUST be untyped constants (array size `[MAX_CHUNK_SIZE]u8` needs compile-time const). (i) `size_of(^u8)` can mis-infer type; `maxsize: c.size_t = 256` safer. (j) `xstrlcpy` in os_lang.odin has `cstring` sig — call with `transmute(cstring)(...)`. `xstrdup`/`xmemdupz`/`xstrlcat`/`vim_strchr` re-reserved via `foreign _`. (k) `do_os_system` uses a labeled block `dos:` with `events` declared before the block so `break dos` works.
     - **Core insight (lowers risk):** the heart `do_os_system()` (shell.c:864) already reuses the Odin-ported process machinery — it calls `libuv_proc_init`, `proc_spawn`, `proc_wait`, `rstream_init`/`rstream_start`, `multiqueue_new_child`, `loop_poll_events`, `wstream_init`/`wstream_new_buffer`/`wstream_write`/`wstream_set_write_cb` (all live `@(export)` Odin procs in `libuv_proc.odin`/`proc.odin`/`rstream.odin`/`wstream.odin`/`multiqueue.odin`/`loop.odin`). So the port is translating C control flow + wiring `foreign _` FFI to C-only helpers, NOT re-implementing process management.
     - **C-only helpers needing `foreign _` FFI (declared in shell.odin, reserved onto C symbols):** msg/screen — `msg_puts`, `msg_putchar`, `msg_start`, `msg_outtrans`, `msg_no_more`, `msg_end`, `msg_sb_eol`, `msg_ext_set_kind`, `msg_ext_no_fast`, `msg_show`, `msg_schedule_semsg`, `smsg`, `emsg`, `emsg_silent`, `msg_outnum`; UI — `ui_flush`, `ui_busy_start`, `ui_busy_stop`, `ui_attach`, `ui_has`; misc — `os_strerror`, `vim_tempname`, `vim_strsave_escaped_ext`, `vim_strnsave_unquoted`, `vim_snprintf`, `set_vim_var_nr`; globals — `lines_left` (int), `no_wait_return` (bool), `msg_no_more` (int); `got_int` (bool) ALREADY linked in `time.odin`; `os_shell_is_cmdexe` ALREADY in `os_env_b.odin`.
     - **Struct ABI (CRITICAL — same class of bug as the `Stream` reorder that caused the `term_delayed_free` spin):** `StringBuilder`/`kv_*` output buffer must mirror C's `kvec_t` (KV_INITIAL_VALUE, `kv_push`/`kv_destroy`/`kv_size`) field-for-field. Verify every offset with `__builtin_offsetof` before trusting. The static helpers defined INSIDE shell.c move with the port (no FFI): `system_data_cb`, `out_data_decide_throttle`, `out_data_ring`, `out_data_event`, `out_data_append_to_screen`, `out_data_cb`, `save_patterns`, `have_wildcard`, `have_dollars`, `tokenize`, `word_length`, `read_input`, `write_output`, `shell_write_cb`, `shell_xescape_xquote`.
     - **Drop Windows `#ifdef` (`MSWin`/`MSWIN`) branches** (Linux-only port, matching prior ports).

    - **DONE (verified 2026-07): Port `os/input.c` (656 lines) → `src/odin/input.odin`** — 16 `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols (`input_start`, `input_stop`, `input_get`, `os_char_avail`, `os_breakcheck`, `line_breakcheck`, `fast_breakcheck`, `veryfast_breakcheck`, `os_isatty`, `input_available`, `input_enqueue_raw`, `input_enqueue`, `input_enqueue_mouse`, `input_blocking`, `os_input_ready`) + private helpers (`cursorhold_event`/`create_cursorhold_event`/`reset_cursorhold_wait`/`try_read`/`input_space`/`check_multiclick`/`handle_mouse_event`/`inbuf_poll`/`input_read_cb`/`process_ctrl_c`/`push_event_key`/`read_error_exit`/`pending_events`). Reuses `rstream_init_fd`/`rstream_start`/`rstream_stop`/`rstream_may_close`, `loop_poll_events`, `multiqueue_*`, `os_hrtime`/`os_realtime`, `apply_autocmds`/`set_vim_var_nr`/`preserve_exit`. `foreign _` FFI to C-only: `trans_special`, `trigger_cursorhold`, `before_blocking`, `typebuf_changed`, `get_real_state`, `getout`, `ins_compl_autocomplete_pending`/`_elapsed`/`ins_compl_active`, `prof_input_start`/`_end`, + globals `used_stdin`/`current_ui`/`did_cursorhold`/`typebuf_was_filled`/`ctrl_c_interrupts`/`mapped_ctrl_c`/`mouse_grid`/`mouse_row`/`mouse_col`/`p_acl`/`p_mouset`/`ch_before_blocking_events`/`typebuf`. Removed the `os_input_ready` reservation (time.odin) and `input_start` reservation (main.odin). `src/nvim/os/input.c` moved to `src/nvim/os/bak/input.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. **This completes the non-Windows `os/` layer port.** Gate + runtime pass: `--version`, `-u NONE -c qa!`, `feedkeys("iabc<Esc>")` (input_enqueue), pty TUI keypress (`script`, input_read_cb→input_enqueue_raw→input_get), `jobwait([j])` **indefinite + timeout**, `system()`, `jobstart`+on_stdout, `termopen`, RPC socket eval (`--remote-expr '1+2'`→3), full LazyVim `-u init.lua --headless -c qa!` — all exit 0. **Gotchas (this Odin build, dev-2026-07):** (a) `UV_TTY`(=14), `silent_mode`/`Rows`/`Columns`/`p_ut`/`curbuf` (main.odin `foreign nvim` block), `uv_guess_handle` (uv_defs.odin), `State`/`do_profiling`/`PROF_YES` (shell.odin) are ALREADY declared package-wide — do NOT redeclare (single Odin package). (b) `TriState` defined as `enum c.int {kNone=-1, kFalse=0, kTrue=1}`. (c) `typebuf_T` mirrored with `#assert size_of==48 && offset_of(tb_len)==24`; only `tb_len` is read. (d) `curbuf->b_mapped_ctrl_c` read via hardcoded offset **12504** (verified `__builtin_offsetof` through a `char probe[offsetof(...)]` + `nm -S`) since `buf_T` is huge — pattern matches shell.odin's `curbuf_field_lnum`. (e) `input_read_cb`/`cursorhold_event` are Odin-calling-convention (passed as `stream_read_cb`/`argv_callback`), not `proc "c"`. (f) file-static state (`input_buffer`/`input_read_pos`/`input_write_pos`/multiclick + push_event_key statics) modeled as `@(private="file")` globals; read/write cursors lazily initialized to `&input_buffer[0]` (Odin package globals can't be initialized to another global's address at declaration). (g) cursorhold uses option-(b): `event_create(cursorhold_event)` + `multiqueue_put_event(main_loop.events, ev)` (C's `multiqueue_put` is a variadic macro). (h) `inbuf_poll` reimplements `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS_UNTIL(&main_loop, NULL, ms, os_input_ready(events)||input_eof)` — drain `main_loop.events` before polling each iteration.
     - **FIXED (surfaced by jobwait, but a pre-existing `proc.odin` bug): `jobwait([j])` with NO timeout (indefinite, `ms=-1`) hung forever.** `f_jobwait` (funcs.c) reparents each job's queue to a `waiting_jobs` MultiQueue and calls `proc_wait(&proc, -1, waiting_jobs)`. C's `proc_wait` uses `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS_UNTIL(proc->loop, events, ms, cond)` where the macro's `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS(loop, multiqueue, timeout)` = **if `multiqueue` has events, `multiqueue_process_events(multiqueue)` (NO poll); else `loop_poll_events(loop, timeout)`**. Our Odin `proc_wait` called bare `loop_poll_events(pr.loop, ms)` which only drains `loop.events`, NEVER the passed `ev`/`waiting_jobs` queue — so the job's exit event (queued to `waiting_jobs`) was never processed, `refcount` never reached 1, infinite spin. `system()` worked because it passes `events=NULL` (→ `ev=proc->events`, a child of `loop.events` reachable via parent link) and jobwait-with-timeout worked because the `ms>=0` branch breaks on timeout. **Fix:** added `loop_process_events_q(loop, q, ms)` (exact mirror of C `LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS`) and rewrote all 3 `proc_wait` poll sites (refcount==0 fast path, main wait loop w/ the macro's `remaining`/`before` timeout bookkeeping, and the got_int cleanup) to use it with the correct `ev` queue. **LESSON: when a C macro takes a queue arg (`LOOP_PROCESS_EVENTS*`), the Odin port MUST thread that queue through and process IT (not just `loop.events`) — a child queue reparented elsewhere is NOT reachable by draining `loop.events` alone.**

  - **DONE (verified 2026-07): Port `mark.c` (marks/jumplist/changelist, 2006 lines) → `src/odin/mark.odin`** — all 49 `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols from `build/include/mark.h.generated.h` (`setmark`/`setmark_pos`/`setpcmark`/`checkpcmark`/`get_jumplist`/`get_changelist`/`mark_get*`/`mark_move_to`/`mark_view_*`/`getnextmark`/`fmarks_check_names`/`mark_check*`/`clrallmarks`/`fm_getname`/`ex_marks`/`ex_delmarks`/`ex_jumps`/`ex_clearjumps`/`ex_changes`/`mark_adjust*`/`mark_col_adjust`/`cleanup_jumplist`/`copy_jumplist`/`mark_*_iter`/`mark_set_*`/`free_jumplist`/`set_last_cursor`/`free_all_marks`/`mark_mb_adjustpos`/`get_buf_local_marks`/`get_raw_global_mark`/`get_global_marks`) + `@(export)` global `namedfm[36]` (shada.c links it). Statics moved as file-private: `do_markset_autocmd`, `fname2fnum`, `fmarks_check_one`, `mark_line`, `show_one_mark`(+`did_title`), `switch_to_mark_buf`, `next_buffer_mark`, `add_mark`, `pos_to_mark`'s `fms`, `mark_move_to`'s `fm_copy`. `src/nvim/mark.c` moved to `bak/mark.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. **Offsets probe-verified** (cc + offsetof against real headers, `-I build/src/nvim/auto -I src -I build/include -I build/cmake.config -I .deps/usr/include`): pos_T=12, fmarkv_T=8, fmark_T=40 {mark@0,fnum@12,timestamp@16,view@24,additional_data@32}, xfmark_T=48 {fmark@0,fname@40}, visualinfo_T=32 {vi_mode@24}, taggy_T=64 {tagname@0,fmark@8,cur_match@48,cur_fnum@52}, WinInfo.wi_mark@8, tabpage_T{tp_next@8,tp_firstwin@40}, exarg_T{arg@0,forceit@76}; win_T: w_buffer@8,w_next@112,w_cursor@136,w_old_cursor_lnum@168,w_old_visual_lnum@180,w_topline@364,w_topfill@372,w_skipcol@388,w_pcmark@4296,w_prev_pcmark@4308,w_jumplist@4320,w_jumplistlen@9120,w_jumplistidx@9124,w_changelistidx@9128,w_tagstack@9152,w_tagstacklen@10436,w_tagstackidx@10432; buf_T: handle/b_fnum@0,b_ml.ml_line_count@8,b_ffname@160,b_wininfo(kvec)@288,b_namedm@384,b_visual@1424,b_last_cursor@1464,b_last_insert@1504,b_last_change@1544,b_changelist@1584,b_changelistlen@5584,b_op_start@7704,b_op_end@7728,b_has_qf_entry@10168,b_prompt_start@11224. **Verified:** 13/13 `test_marks.vim` Test_* funcs run clean post-cutover (Test_delmarks SIGSEGV + getmarklist 'z'-names bugs found & fixed pre-cutover); jumplist/changelist/mark_adjust parity vs system nvim; `--version`/`-u NONE -c qa!`/full LazyVim headless exit 0. **Gotchas:** (a) **C `EXTERN const char e_invarg[]` arrays must NOT be linked as Odin `cstring` globals** — the symbol IS the array, so Odin loads its first 8 bytes ("E474: In") as the pointer → SIGSEGV inside emsg→xstrdup. Define error strings as Odin cstring literals instead (same pattern as digraph.odin). (b) `get_buf_local_marks` passes DISTINCT literals per mark (`S_LEN("''")`, `S_LEN("'\"")`...) — reusing the mutated `mname[3]` buffer made every mark report as `'z`. (c) `MB_PTR_ADV` in this tree is `utfc_ptr2len` — there is NO `mb_ptr2len` symbol. (d) `tv_list_alloc` takes `ptrdiff_t` — main.odin's decl was widened to `c.ssize_t`. (e) Suffixed local FFI names need explicit `@(link_name=...)` or the linker looks for the suffixed symbol. (f) `for i := 0` loops passed to C.int params must be typed `for i: C.int = 0`. (g) Oldtest harness (`runnvim.sh`) hangs with our binary (uses `--not-a-term`-era runner + :terminal); run tests directly: `nvim_odin -u NONE -i NONE --headless --cmd 'set noswapfile noundofile nomore' -c 'source test_marks.vim' -c 'call Test_x()'` and check `v:errors`; compare against `/usr/bin/nvim` for parity but beware version drift (system nvim differs from THIS tree on `gqG` empty-buffer '[ marks — ours matches this tree's expectations).
  - **DONE (verified 2026-07): Port `digraph.c` (digraph + keymap, ~2218 lines) → `src/odin/digraph.odin`** — all `@(export)` `proc "c"` symbols: `digraph_get`/`digraph_set`/`digraph_getlist`/`f_digraph_get`/`f_digraph_set`/`f_digraph_getlist`/`f_digraph_setlist`/`getexactdigraph`/`get_digraph`/`get_digraph_for_char`/`printdigraph`/`listdigraphs`/`do_digraph`/`ex_digraph`/`keymap_init`/`ex_loadkeymap`/`keymap_unload`/`keymap_ga_clear`/`get_keymap_str`/`buf_p_keymap` + globals `digraphdefault[1366]` (generated) and `header_table[27]`. Keymap API (`keymap_set`/`keymap_get`/`keymap`) does NOT exist as Vim functions in this nvim version (confirmed `exists('*keymap')=0` on system nvim); only internals were in `digraph.c` — all ported. `src/nvim/digraph.c` moved to `src/nvim/bak/digraph.c`; `cmake --build build --target libnvim` + relink. **Perfect parity vs system `/usr/bin/nvim`:** 29-digraph `digraph_get` sweep all match; `digraph_set`+`digraph_get` roundtrip (`xy`→Z 5a); `digraph_getlist(1)`=1366 entries, empty pair-diff; `:digraphs`/`:digraphs!` no crash; `:set keymap=accents` no crash (matches ref `b:keymap_name`). Full LazyVim headless `-es -c qa!` exits 0; `-u NONE -c qa!`/`--version` exit 0. **Gotchas:** (a) `tv_list_append_string` C arg is `const ssize_t len` (64-bit) → declare `C.ssize_t` not `C.int` (32-bit ABI mismatch caused SIGSEGV on `digraph_getlist`). (b) `digraphdefault` Odin array has NO `{NUL,NUL,NUL}` terminator (unlike C static) — iterate `for i := 0; i < len(digraphdefault)`, not `for dp.char1 != 0` (terminator reliance read 6 past-end spurious entries). (c) `digraph_get` returns a **string**, not a number — callers `char2nr()` it. (d) `-es` stdout shows `Done!!` (pre-existing quirk); verify via `writefile` to a file, not `echo`/`put`. (e) `redir! > file` creates unreadable perms in this sandbox — prefer `writefile()`.
  11. **Phase 4: Core Editor Subsystems** — `os/` + `event/` layers are fully ported (164 C files remain: the editor core). (2) e_invarg string must be "E474: Invalid argument" not E513 — fixed everywhere. (3) STR2NR_ALL = 0x0f (BIN|OCT|HEX|OOCT) not 0x07 — 0o12 octal parsing. (4) option_value2string: wc_use_keyname leaves *wcp UNSET for non-wildchar opts (wc stays 0 → snprintf path), and NULL-string guard added. (5) **CRITICAL ABI LESSON #3: C functions taking sctx_T BY VALUE must be declared with the struct type, NOT rawptr** — last_set_msg_r/set_option_sctx_r now take sctx_T by value; passing rawptr made C read our pointer as sc_sid (garbage sid=heap-ptr). (6) OPT_GLOBAL=0x1, OPT_LOCAL=0x2, OPT_MODELINE=0x4 (I had GLOBAL=4 → setglobal/setlocal both treated as scope_both). (7) exarg_T.cmdidx is at offset **64**, not 0 (arg@0, forceit@76). (8) copy_opt_sctx: buf_opt_idx table REGENERATED as Odin literal [92]C.int from options_enum.generated.h (foreign bind to main.c.o's R symbol gave garbage); kBufOptTabstop=80 not 90 (ts/vts copy used 90 for both). (9) win_alloc now calls nvim_odin_init_winopt(new_wp) (new C helper in option.c running check_winopt on both opt structs) — window string opts must be non-NULL before get_varp_from derefs them during startup defaults. (10) get_scriptname: added `case 0: return ""` (sc_sid 0 = never-set crashed SCRIPT_ITEM(0)). (11) optval_free: skip strings with size==0 (empty_string_option aliasing guard). (12) REMAINING FAILURES: Test_rightleftcmd fails IDENTICALLY on ref (env); Test_set_completion comma-escape cases (Xbar,Xcom delimiter scan); Test_options_command (needs VIMRUNTIME at runtime); a u32-widening read bug on some global-local reads ("Expected -1 but got 4294967295" — a 4-byte int field read via 8-byte OptInt load); several CRASH tests = double-free of static/default strings in optval_free path (size>0 guard reduced but not eliminated); Test_set_option_errors nondeterministic snprintf crash on errmsg. NEXT: fix u32-widening + double-free cluster, then full battery + real cutover (delete option.c, keep shims in a new option_shim.c hosting statics: p_term/p_ttytype/p_*_nobin/nopaste, options[] table include, switch/restore_option_context, vimoption2dict, put_c, apply_optionset_autocmd, option_expand wrappers, set_init_1/2/3 which are still C).

  - **option.odin KNOWN-ISSUE FIXES (2026-08-25): sweep now 75/85 PASS, 0 crashes (was ~52 pass with 14 crashes). Fixed this round:** (1) **copy_winopt was copying ALL wo_* fields as 4-byte ints** — OptInt(8B) fields (wo_scr@224, wo_nuw@168, wo_lhi@200, wo_cole@328, wo_siso@352, wo_so@360, wo_sop@368, wo_fdl@64, wo_fdl_save@72, wo_fml@96, wo_fdn@104) got truncated → `&l:scrolloff` read 4294967295 instead of -1; split scalar list into int[26] + int64[11]. (2) **shell.odin msg_no_more declared c.int but C is EXTERN bool (1 byte)** — writing it as int clobbered adjacent ex_nesting_level@0x6b0ee0 (BSS neighbors: didset_vim/0x6b0edc, didset_vimruntime/dd, msg_no_more/ede, ex_nesting_level/e0!) → nesting went negative → spurious "Entering Debug mode" hangs after any `:!cmd` or system() call. LESSON: audit EVERY foreign var decl size against C's actual type (bool=1 vs int=4); nm -S shows symbol sizes to verify. (3) e_trailing_cstr foreign var bound C's `EXTERN char e_trailing[]` ARRAY as cstring → loaded first 8 bytes of "E488: T..." as pointer → snprintf crash in do_set error formatter; replaced all such uses with Odin literals (also removed unused e_unknown_option2_g binding, and spell.odin's e_invarg_cstr same bug). (4) Removed Odin set_option_sctx export entirely — its FFI decl + export same-name suppressed emission, so calls bound to C's weak copy; now set_option_sctx_r FFI binds directly to C's real function. (5) get_scriptname: added `case 0: return ""` for sc_sid==0 (SCRIPT_ITEM(-1) crash on :verbose for never-set options). (6) option_value2string: NULL string guard + wc starts at 0 (wc_use_keyname leaves *wcp untouched for non-wildchar opts). **REMAINING test_options.vim failures (75/85):** ref-parity env drift: rightleftcmd/shortmess_F2/opt_scrolljump/opt_cmdheight/set_completion_string_values fail IDENTICALLY on /usr/bin/nvim. Genuinely ours (5): options_command (needs $VIMRUNTIME at runtime for optwin), set_completion (comma-escape delimiter scan "./Xbar,Xcom"), set_min_lines_columns (v:errmsg empty after let &columns=0 / setbufvar paths though emsg fires — v:errmsg cleared between emsg and add()), string_option_revert_on_failure (tv_to_optval rejects LIST for comma-string opts like spellfile), binary_depending_options ("Last set from" shows Xafter.vim instead of the vimrc — sctx scope tracking for binary-dependent opts). **UPDATE (2026-08-25 session 2): 4 of the 5 fixed — sweep now 77/85 PASS, 0 crashes, backupskip + min_lines_columns + string_option_revert_on_failure + binary_depending_options all pass. Fixes:** (A) **valid_spellfile in spell.odin was the WRONG function** — I'd ported an rtp-existence check instead of optionstr.c's validator (copy_option_part loop; each part must be ≥4 chars ending ".add", all chars vim_is_fname_char); rewrote with copy_option_part_r + new vim_is_fname_char_r FFI. LESSON: same C name can exist in different upstream files — verify semantics against the CALLER's expectations (optionstr.c did_set_spellfile), not just the signature. (B) **digraph.odin ex_loadkeymap read exarg fields at wrong offsets**: `(^LineGetter)(eap)^` reads eap+0 (=arg!) not ea_getline@168; cookie is @176 not 0xc0(=192). E105 "not in a sourced file" for every :loadkeymap → keymap files never loaded. (C) **digraph.odin buf_p_keymap offset 23616 wrong — real b_p_keymap@10792**; also kmap_state real 7856 (not 7872), b_kmap_ga real 7864 (not 15744!). And the p_keymap read needed DOUBLE indirection: slot at curbuf+10792 holds char*; `p_keymap_slot := (^^u8)(uintptr(b)+10792); p_keymap := p_keymap_slot^`. These wrong offsets made `set keymap=greek` fail E544 with garbage name "@\%qRV". (D) **set_options_bin missing didset_options_sctx(opt_flags, p_bin_dep_opts)** tail call — binary-dependent options' "Last set from" attribution was lost; added nvim_odin_didset_options_sctx + nvim_odin_get_p_bin_dep_opts shims in option.c and call it from my set_options_bin. (E) **handle_tty/set_option_value errmsg stability**: errmsg points into a proc-static errbuf that the next call overwrites while vars.c still displays it; handle_tty now memcpy's ret into its own stable static buffer before returning. **REMAINING failures (7, see session-3 update below for reclassification):** ref-parity: rightleftcmd/shortmess_F2/opt_scrolljump/opt_cmdheight/set_completion_string_values (+backupskip passes standalone, flaky under sweep due to `silent !` child spawn timing). Genuinely ours (3): options_command (optwin buffer opens but window focus/layout differs — bufname('') empty after wincmd k), set_completion (comma-escape delimiter scan "./Xbar,Xcom" in set_context_in_set_cmd second backslash-counting loop), opt_cmdheight (Expected ht-1 got ht-2 — clamp off by one vs laststatus). **UPDATE (session 4): 2 more fixed — options_command + set_min_lines_columns now pass; sweep 78/85 PASS, backupskip passes standalone (flaky under sweep due to parent `silent !` child spawn timing). Fixes:** (F) **kOptFlagComma was 1<<6 (real 1<<10), kOptFlagFlagList was 1<<7 (real 1<<13)** — comma-delimiter scan in set_context_in_set_cmd now splits commalist opts correctly. (G) **os_getperm returned >=0 for nonexistent files** — it mixed raw-syscall stat (linux.stat does NOT set C errno) with posix.errno() which returned STALE values; uv_translate_sys_error(stale-0)=0 → perm=0 → readfile treated URL-named buffers as non-regular ("is not a file" → :new nvim-optwin://optwin created the buffer but aborted before opening a window). Fixed: return UV_ENOENT directly from the _do_stat failure path. NOTE: other os_* wrappers (_c_open etc) are safe — they posix.set_errno() from the linux.* Errno return. **REMAINING failures (7):** ref-parity drift: rightleftcmd/shortmess_F2/opt_scrolljump/opt_cmdheight/set_completion_string_values fail identically on /usr/bin/nvim; backupskip flaky-under-sweep only (passes standalone). Genuinely ours (2): options_command — INTERMITTENT (passed latest standalone run; occasionally optwin buffer opens without its window when an unrelated empty-emsg aborts mid-flow); set_completion line 140 — `set invtabstop=<Tab>` inserts literal TAB: EXPAND_NOTHING → ExpandOne FAIL → our ex_getln self-inserts the char where ref discards it (INPUT-LAYER nuance K_TAB vs p_wc delivery, NOT options code; getcompletion parity confirmed []). **UPDATE (session 5): ALL genuinely-ours failures FIXED — test_options.vim now 80/85 PASS, 0 crashes; the 5 remaining fails are REF-PARITY (fail identically on /usr/bin/nvim: rightleftcmd/shortmess_F2/opt_scrolljump/opt_cmdheight/set_completion_string_values). Final fix:** (H) **set_context_in_set_cmd was missing C's `arg = p` after the no/inv prefix strip** — find_option_len searched "invtabstop" (no such option → -1 → NOTHING→return) leaving ctx=EXPAND_BOOL_SETTINGS(5), so ExpandOne proceeded with BOOL context and the <Tab> fell through as a literal char insert. With `arg = p`, "tabstop" resolves → NUMBER passes bool-check → pattern non-NUL → EXPAND_NOTHING → FAIL → CMDLINE_NOT_CHANGED (char discarded, matching ref). LESSON: when porting pointer-cursor functions, EVERY rebinding of the local cursor (arg/p/s) must be replicated — an missed rebind silently changes what later lookups search. options_command also passes reliably now (verified 5/5 runs). **CUTOVER ATTEMPT (session 5): deleting option.c entirely requires porting ~53 more symbols** — the nvim_odin_* shims (opt_table/varp accessors, p_*_nobin/nopaste getters+setters, switch/restore_option_context, vimoption2dict, put_c, didset_options_sctx, option_expand, find_option_len, apply_optionset_autocmd, change/clear helpers, init_winopt), the statics they wrap (options[] table via options.generated.h include, p_*_nobin/nopaste, p_term/p_ttytype, expand_option_* statics, p_vfile def which is #ifdef IN_OPTION_C in option_vars.h), set_init_1/2/3 + their static helpers (shell_rules, set_init_default_shell/expand_env/fenc_default, change_option_and_default_if_unset), get_varp/get_opt_idx statics — PLUS consolidating all cross-file duplicate FFI decls of now-exported symbols (shortmess declared 3×: search.odin shortmess_s + undo.odin shortmess_r + option.odin export; same for others → mangled `.8` refs when option.c's definitions vanish). DECISION: keep the cutover-lite pattern (option.c with 89 `#pragma weak` symbols; Odin strong defs win) — it is stable, fully tested, and avoids the large consolidation risk. Real cutover can proceed later by creating option_shim.c hosting only statics/table/shims/set_init_* and deduplicating FFI decls.

BUGS FIXED DURING VERIFY: (1) regmatch split across the proc boundary — engine used a fresh zeroed regmatch (nil regprog → SIGSEGV); fix: pass ^Regmmatch_T through (LESSON: when splitting a C function, thread ALL shared state explicitly — the compiler can't catch a valid-but-empty struct). My replaceAll for &regmatch→regmatch also broke ex_global's two correct sites — fixed back; LESSON: replaceAll across same-named locals in two procs needs a post-grep audit. (2) Length-query dest must be sub_firstline.data, NOT nil (E38 — I misread C). (3) REGSUB_* values ROTATED (real COPY=1/MAGIC=2/BACKSLASH=4 — caused phantom "not enough space"). LESSON: verify every enum against its C header, never from memory. (4) Dropped `sub_firstlnum = lnum` per-line init (ml_get(0) garbage → crash). (5) Per-line kvec destroy freed storage but kept stale len=0/cap=8 → next line skipped grow → nil write SIGSEGV; reset ALL THREE (C's kvec is per-line fresh). (6) prev_matchcol init must be MAXCOL, not 0 (broke %s/^$/). (7) PRE-EXISTING search.odin BUG: CMOD_KEEPPATTERNS was 0x080 (=CONFIRM!) instead of 0x1000 — :keeppatterns never suppressed saves; fixed (audited all other CMOD_* values, all correct). (8) No goto/labels in Odin — `skip:` label became a fall-through tail with `skipped` flags. (9) proc "c" has NO context and this build's append/delete take no allocator — manual kvec (xmalloc/xrealloc/xfree + len/cap, Batch-16 idiom) for line_matches/preview lists. (10) More FFI-vs-export collisions removed (no_u_sync→undo.odin, syn_check_group→option.odin, concat_str→spell.odin, coladvance→undo.odin, ml_replace_buf→fold.odin, search_match_*→search.odin, Lpos_T→search.odin). (11) My own stray edit deleted a push_preview_o body line — reverted immediately + rebuilt to verify (EDIT-THEN-VERIFY discipline for every touch, even reverts).
